## Estado

### Metrics

Sixteen metrics. `Baseline` is measured at `main` @ `6701be00`; `Now` moves only with the pull
request that moved it, and `Moved by` names that pull request by its review alias (`—`: not moved
since the baseline). `PR cp1b` is unit C-P1b and `PR pdeps` the patched-dependencies gate. A `PR`
followed by a capital letter and a number — `PR R9a`, `PR R10b` — is the
[§Plan (fixed)](TESTING_REFOUNDATION_PLAN.md#plan-fixed)'s label for a work unit's pull request (`R`
Phase 1, `V` Phase 3, `E` Phase 4b, `C` Phase 5, `X` the runner experiment), a trailing letter
naming one of its parts. [§Log](#log) gives each alias its branch.

**How the baselines were obtained.** M1, M5, M7 and M8 are derived from the tree and reproduced
byte-for-byte by `scripts/testing/metrics.mjs`, pinned by
`apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/testingMetrics.test.ts`. M5 is derived since PR R10b: its orphans are the
quarantine's entries plus every file the reach engine reports unreached outside it, and its second
figure is the files the engine reports collected more than once. M15's first half was measured with
`gh issue list --label nightly-failure --state open` (31); its second half was measured by counting
the distinct `(image, POSTGRES_DB)` pairs across `services.postgres` in `.github/workflows/` (4) and
becomes derivable when WU-4b.7 lands `test-contracts identity --json`. M2, M9, M10, M11 and M12 come
from a runner summary or a named run, which is why their cells say so: the script prints their
source rather than inventing a number. M3, M4, M6, M13, M14 and M16 depend on artefacts that do not
exist at the baseline commit (`eslint-suppressions.json`, `ledger.json`, coverage summaries), so the
script prints `—` with that reason; their baselines are the counted upper bounds from the plan's
Appendix C and the measuring report.

**M2.** Its numbers are pasted from runner summaries: the vitest `passed` and `todo` totals of a full
run, and the node:test total of the TIER summary; Playwright in CI stays 0 until WU-6.E8 lands its
job. In CI each vitest shard prints the `default` reporter beside `blob` (`workspaceReporters()`,
`packages/vitest-shared`), so a failing shard names its file, test and diff in the job log.

**M3.** It counts the suppressed violations of the three `testing/*` block rules in
`eslint-suppressions.json`. Its target `0` is the change's end, not Phase 2's: by the Product Owner's
decision of 2026-11-06 (L41), 9 suppressions stay held after WU-2.10, each with the card of
`docs/product/MASTER_PLAN.md` §5.13 that removes it: DC-1 5 (`performanceMonitor.init.test.ts`), DC-4 1
(`auditLogger.test.ts`), DC-8(b) 2 (`TikTokAdapter.test.ts`) and DC-11 1 (`engagementPredictor.test.ts`),
measured on 2026-11-07.

**M8.** Its first figure counts the tracked `vitest.config.*` files that declare a `coverage` block
over every tracked `vitest.config.*`, so a new config moves the denominator and the target reads
`all / all`, not a count. The floors are the global `lines` thresholds: their minimum and median
once two configs carry one, and `apps/api`'s. WU-5.1 puts coverage defaults in the shared factory,
and WU-5.9 makes every package measured or listed with a reason.

### Work units

✅ done · ⛔ blocked. `Evidence` is a run id, a checksum, or the pull request's own evidence block.

there. Row 0.16 landed ahead of Node 26's LTS date on 2026-10-15 ([D52](#decisions-log)) and stays 🔄
until task 0.16.3 moves the homelab host and the local worktrees.

[toolchain-lag]: TESTING_REFOUNDATION_MEASUREMENTS.md#toolchain-lag-wu-t4a

### Gates

Every new or modified gate lands with its red demonstrated on the COMPLETE step — never on the body
of a heredoc, where the `exit 1` lives in the step's shell and a node or python body exits 0 on its
own. A row is added by the pull request that adds or modifies the gate, and `Red proof` carries the
exact command, the observed non-zero exit, and the byte-exact restore evidence.

| Gate                                                                                                                                                 | Kind (ESLint / fitness / job)                                                                                                                                          | Red proof (command, exit, restore)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | PR                                                             |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Testing toolchain holds (WU-T.4f, ADR-0036): a lag past the 30-day budget has a documented hold; a younger lag is reported                           | CI step in the `Dependency Consistency` job of `fitness.yml`, running `scripts/testing/holds-gate.mjs` with `::error` paired to `exit 1` (fitness #34 PAIRED)          | run block extracted from the workflow; with the vitest hold row removed from `SECURITY_CANON.md` → exit 1 naming `vitest`, `@vitest/coverage-v8`, `@vitest/ui` (installed 4.1.11, target 5.0.1); file restored, `sha256sum -c` OK; re-run → exit 0 (52 measured, 43 lags, 0 violations). **Modified 2026-09-28 by `refound/0-holds-table-fix`** — the parser now locates `Remove-when` by HEADER NAME, so the red had to be re-proven on the same complete run block: with the `tsx` row's remove-when cell BLANKED → exit 1 naming `tsx` and "carries no remove-when"; restored from a byte copy, `sha256sum -c` OK and `cmp` identical; re-run → exit 0 (52 measured, 41 lags, 0 violations) **Modified 2026-10-14 by `workstream/dependency-update-policy`** ([ADR-0036](../technical/ADR-0036-dependency-update-cadence.md)): a lag is red only once the oldest stable release above the installed version has been mature for 30 days, and a row that describes a scheduled hold is refused. On the COMPLETE step, its `run:` body extracted with the `yaml` package from the workflow as changed (`sha256sum` `3c023211…f90f11`) and run with `bash --noprofile --norc -eo pipefail` from the worktree root at 2026-10-14T20:45Z, on the canon as changed (`0e764622…`): the tree exits 0, `50 population packages measured, 14 below latest mature (4 within budget, 10 held, 0 undocumented)`; (1) `Scheduled, not blocked` planted in the `msw` row → exit 1, the gate naming that row as a scheduled hold, under the `::error` annotation; (2) the `typescript` row deleted → exit 1, `typescript` `lag: UNDOCUMENTED` with 7.0.2 mature for 30 days or more, under the annotation; after each the canon restored from a byte copy, `sha256sum -c` OK and `cmp` identical; re-run → exit 0. Before the canon edit the new gate exited 1 on the real table, naming its six scheduled rows. `holdsGate.test.ts` went from 21 to 30 tests: 7 of the new cases fail on the previous gate and pass on this one (the 29-day lag, the second before the budget ends, the oldest unmet release, the day count, the summary split, the two scheduled rows), and four mutants of the gate (the retired words matched case-sensitively, the days counted from the newest release, the budget clock started by the newest release, the refusal dropped) each turn it red, each restore matching its `sha256sum`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `refound/0-toolchain-holds-gate` + `refound/0-holds-table-fix` |

(3) the catalog pin put back to `25.9.3` — the defect this slice closes → exit 1 naming the pin's major against the runtime's; restored, `sha256sum -c` OK (`d1d0af58…4572e`), `cmp` identical; re-run → exit 0. Each clean run prints `98 manifests, engines.node >=24.15.0 <25, runtime 24, @types/node 24.13.6`. The same gate under Node 24.15.0 exits 1, "running on Node 24.15.0 (major 24) while … .nvmrc declares major 26". Task 0.16.2's red, a manifest ahead of a 24 runtime, is in `tasks.md`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `refound/0-toolchain-types-node`                               |
| Hexagonal layer policy (WU-T.4b, slice 0.21, then item (v)): the cruiser's layer rules and the suite that pins them                                  | `pnpm check:architecture` (the cruiser's rules + the shrink-only baseline, reds in the Architecture row); the policy pin `architecturePolicies.test.ts`                | **The policy pin, `PR v-c`** (2026-10-05): `apps/api/tests/unit/lint/architecturePolicies.test.ts`, 23 tests, loads `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` and pins what the shrink-only baseline cannot: the exact rule set, each rule's severity and anchors (`from`, `to`, `pathNot`, `dependencyTypesNot`), and the `options.exclude` scope. A weakened rule leaves a stale entry only where it has entries, so the plant takes rules with none: `workers-no-api` removed, and `core-domain-no-framework`'s `pathNot` widened with `^packages/ports/`. On the COMPLETE command `pnpm check:architecture` still exits 0 (`✔ no dependency violations found (1711 modules, 7488 dependencies cruised)`, 138 known violations ignored), while the suite exits 1 with 3 failures naming both rules: `workers-no-api is missing from the config`, `core-domain-no-framework: to`, and the rule-set check. The config went back byte-exact, its `sha256sum` matching, and the suite passes 23/23. The layer rules' own red proofs are in the Architecture row. **History**: until 2026-10-05 this row was the `boundaries/dependencies` rule of `eslint-plugin-boundaries` 7.2.0 (slice 0.21: nine probe files, 86 imports, `pnpm lint` exit 1 with 35 errors), retired in `PR v-c` with SMELL-183, together with its pinning suite `boundariesPolicies.test.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `workstream/eslint-boundaries-policies` · `PR v-c`             |

(2) `"gaxios@7": 7.1.5` deleted, orphaning its allowlist entry → exit 1 naming the entry and quoting the reason that now excuses nothing — the orphan-row class the holds gate still cannot see, closed here from birth. (3) this slice's own normalization regressed to `"valibot@<=1.4.1"` → exit 1 naming the inclusive bound and the canonical rewrite. After each: restored from a byte copy, `sha256sum -c` OK and `cmp` identical, `git status --porcelain` clean of it, re-run → exit 0. `metrics.mjs --m7` counts the data rows of this table: total rows, and rows whose `Red proof` cell is
filled. **A script in the tree is not a gate until a workflow runs it** — the same rule fitness #30
holds over test files: a check no collector names never executes, however complete it looks in the
tree. The holds gate earned its row only with the pull request that landed its step in the
`Dependency Consistency` job, red demonstrated on the COMPLETE step, and M7 moved with it, not before.

Signed as a block by Edward on 2026-09-27. **D17 stayed open until 2026-11-07**, a product
classification, not a technical fork; L42 decided the two CLI seeds it covered DEAD, and both left the
tree. **D24 and D25 precede D26 although they landed after it**: slices `P.1`
(`refound/3-fitness-inventory`, D24) and `0.11` (`refound/0-toolchain-storybook`, D25) numbered their
decisions before `0.17` wrote D26. D24 landed with `P.1` and D25 with `0.11`, so the ids run without a gap.
**D44 is not on `main`**: it lived on a withdrawn branch, and the circular-gate decision it held is
recorded in D43 (e), so the ids run D43 → D45.

take or leave 0.18.6–0.18.8                                        | **(a) Branches.** Commits were made on local `workstream/refound-<slice>` branches and published to the pull requests' `refound/*` names, because the commit gate accepts commits only on `workstream/*` (it has read the right repository since `4fcdc116`); Edward authorized the forced publication of those eleven branches and their merge on 2026-10-02 (drain mode: nothing new is stacked until the stack is in main). **(b) Size.** Where the native review refused a candidate by size (`lens_context_budget_exceeded`), the candidate was reviewed as smaller ones: slice `0.11` is three commits with the tree of the single one. **(c) Pre-registered holds.** A release inside the seven-day buffer that cannot simply be taken, or is simply scheduled, gets its hold row before the lag exists, marked as pre-registered and proven with `node scripts/testing/holds-gate.mjs --instant <iso>`, which evaluates the gate as of that instant: msw 3.x (`2026-10-05T15:45Z`), `@typescript-eslint` 8.71.0 (`2026-10-05T17:09Z`), Storybook 10.6.1 (`2026-10-06T17:14Z`). **(d) Not taken.** Contract tasks 0.18.6–0.18.8 ("slice to be named at the rebase") are separate scopes, and drain mode stacks nothing new. **Who decided:** Edward authorized the forced publication and the merge, in drain mode; the orchestrator chose the local branch naming in (a) and decided (b)–(d), all reported to him in the morning summary                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | —      | 2026-10-02 |
| D29 | Slice `0.22`: demolish the admin Storybook, or measure, keep what runs, remove what does not, backfill runnable stories, stories part of creating UI       | demolish the admin Storybook (the 2026-10-01 plan) · measure first, keep what runs, remove what does not, backfill a runnable story per component, and require one for every new component              | Edward, 2026-10-02: "el plan no es removerlo, es medir lo actual y ver si funciona". **(a) Runner.** Step zero is a story runner the canon allows; Jest is forbidden. The candidate to measure is `@storybook/nextjs-vite` + `@storybook/addon-vitest` on vitest browser mode with Playwright, which Storybook documents as the test-runner's successor for Vite-powered frameworks. **(b) Measure first.** The first measured step builds the client Storybook and runs its 58 stories in headless Chromium — render, page errors, console errors, axe WCAG 2.1 A/AA — and tries the admin Storybook, which has 0 stories. **(c) Keep or remove.** What works stays; what does not is removed. **(d) Backfill.** Component by component: a component without a story gets one, a component with a story has it run, and a passing story moves the work to the next component — `packages/ui` first, then the client, then admin, in reviewable slices. **(e) Passes / fails.** A story passes when, over the component's meaningful states, it shows no page error, no console error and zero axe violations in a real browser. A failing story is a defect: it is fixed or tracked, never skipped or suppressed. **(f) Part of creating UI.** The coding standard requires a story for every component, with an ADR; a gate enforces it, as a ratchet during the backfill and hard-zero after it; the agent writes, runs and inspects the story before handing over. **(g) What does not change.** The removal of `@storybook/test-runner` by #336 stands: it had no configuration, no `play` functions, was never invoked and carried Jest. This supersedes the 2026-10-01 demolition. **Landed:** A #425–#427, B #428, C #432, D #439, F #441, G #443, E #445 (on #444); 10.6.1 #447; backfill per [D51](#decisions-log)      | Edward | 2026-10-02 |

| D55 | WU-2.1 classifier: deviations from the plan's literal text                                                                                                 | classify by the plan's literal text · narrow eight rules where the literal text marks restricting tests decorative or floods TITLE-CONTRADICTION                                                        | **Narrow the eight rules** (accepted at review of WU-2.1, 2026-10-19); each is stated in the header of the module that applies it, and every figure is the classifier over the 1,030 tracked test files of `b053ba58`. (1) A title's "rejects" is checked by any restricting assertion and its "throws" by throw evidence or a restricting assertion that names a failure: "rejects" in this repository names a refused request, not a promise; with (2) and (8), TITLE-CONTRADICTION 1,127 → 29. (2) A throw, rejection or status word inside a `when`, `if`, `on`, `after`, `whose`, `given`, `for`, `maps` or `mapping` clause is an input, not a promised outcome. (3) An `if` whose condition an earlier unconditional assertion decided is neither CONDITIONAL nor GUARDED-RETURN (`if (!result.ok) return` after `expect(result.ok).toBe(true)` narrows a type), read through `!`, `&&`, `\|\|`, `=== null`-style forms and an asserted `typeof` since 2026-10-21: EXISTENCE-ONLY 950 → 667, and 2 more. (4) The truthiness of a value named as a boolean predicate (`is…`, `has…`, `includes`, `some`, ...) is BEHAVIOURAL; `result.ok` stays EXISTENCE: EXISTENCE-ONLY 667 → 616. (5) `if (cond) throw` in the block's own body is a BEHAVIOURAL check, not a condition. (6) A method named `expect…` or `assert…` on any receiver (a page object's) and a callback passed by name are OPAQUE: EMPTY 62 → 16. (7) A success print reads `pass` as a word and `success` at a word start, so "password reset" and "unsuccessful" are no success. (8) "returns undefined" or "returns null" is no contradiction when a restricting assertion names null or undefined. | D61 | How CI binds a deleted or rewritten test block to its measured mutation, and which number the gate takes with #54-#55 reserved                             | a gate that re-runs each mutation · a gate that checks the committed record exists and covers every touched block; for the number, take #54 and shift both reservations · wire #56                      | **By the Product Owner (L45, L46, L49): a gate over the committed record, fitness #54; WU-3.13 → #55, WU-4b.7 → #56.** L45: "Add a CI gate that refuses a pull request when a block it deletes or rewrites has no case in the committed record with a GAIN or SAFE verdict. The gate does not re-run the mutations: it checks that the record exists and covers every touched block, and the verifier's re-run covers a forged or stale record." L46: "The gate binds every pull request, with three exclusions:" "a moved block, whose bodyHash reappears at the head, is not refused"; "a node:test block is counted, not refused, until the node:test adapter exists"; "a block whose test imports a source file the same pull request changes is a behaviour change: it is not refused, and it is listed in the gate's summary for review." L49, to the question whether the gate drops its string-literal rule and keeps the exclusion for real imports only: "Yes." So a test that loads a changed source by a path it builds needs its measured record, as any other. Measured at main `05d4b83d`: of the 58 first-parent merges since `ledger.json` was committed (`3ef9bf39`, 2026-10-28), 18 removed or rewrote 695 blocks (687 removed, 8 rewritten; 627 vitest, 68 node:test), and the rename #834 moved 22 whose bodyHash reappears under the new path. | D62 | Whether the truthiness of a verdict (`ok`, `success`) is presence, and what `testing/no-existence-only-test` becomes after its calibration                 | keep the rule as it reads · narrow it to leave verdicts out · drop it                                                                                                                                   | **By the Product Owner (L44): a verdict's truthiness is BEHAVIOURAL, and the rule narrows.** L44: "the classifier no longer counts a verdict (`.success`, `.ok`) as EXISTENCE-ONLY". A verdict is a member named `ok` or `success`, read by a dot or by a string literal, outside a `.mock.` chain; the rule removes the asymmetry under which `expect(r.ok).toBe(true)` and `assert.ok(!r.success)` restricted a value while `expect(r.ok).toBeTruthy()` and `assert.ok(r.success)` did not. Calibration (`mutations/2.1c-calibration.record.json`): of the blocks the narrowing leaves out, 6 of 6 red under a mutation that flips the verdict; of the blocks the rule still reports, 9 of 9 measured green under a mutation that keeps the value present but wrong (1 unmeasured, DEF-187). Neither sample shows the 90 % of task 2.1.2: both were chosen by hand across packages and frameworks, not drawn at random, and even a random sample with no failure bounds the rate only at 72 % for 9 of 9 and 61 % for 6 of 6, one-sided at 95 % confidence. EXISTENCE-ONLY 149 → 46 blocks, M3 155 → 52. **By the Maintainer (L54): the narrowed rule stays at `error` on this measurement, on the condition that the calibration is completed later.** L54: "As long as it gets resolved later, I approve it." - 2026-11-08 — WU-2.3b, `PR d23b` (`workstream/tr-2-3b-mutation-diff`) — the differential mutation tool, by the Product Owner's decision of 2026-11-08 (L44). WU-2.10a accepted 39 rewrites because each went red under a mutation of its subject; run against the base test file, 24 of the 29 blocks the hard probe could not decide were already caught at the base. **The tool.** `node scripts/testing/mutation-diff.mjs --cases <file.json> --out <record.json> [--base <sha>] [--head <ref>] [--only <id>]... [--require gain|safe]`, the root script `mutation-diff`, with its pure core in `scripts/testing/lib/mutation-diff-core.mjs` and its Vite plugin in `scripts/testing/lib/mutation-diff-plugin.mjs`. A case names its test, its blocks by ledger id, its subject, one search replaced at its first occurrence and its replacement; a case file with an unknown key, a missing field, a duplicate id or a block outside its test is refused with exit 2. Each case's scope (the test, the files it names, and every test of the same vitest project that imports the subject directly), computed on each side from that side's tree and recorded per side, runs four times with vitest: the test files at the base and at the head, each unmutated and mutated. The plugin serves each test file's base content, and the head's under `--head`, in memory, mutates the working tree's subject in memory, and writes a marker and a served log, so a run whose mutation never reached the transform is `not-applied`, and one whose served content never did is `not-served`, never a verdict; clean runs are shared between cases with the same side, base and scope. A rewrite is GAIN only when no base scope test is red under the mutation and every listed block is red at the head, NO-GAIN when any base scope test is red, each named, and BROKEN when a listed block stays green at the head; a deletion is SAFE, LOST or VACUOUS; UNDECIDED carries its reason. A rewrite needs its block on both sides, so a new test is `block-missing`, an addition and not a rewrite; a base with no scope file to run is `base-empty`, since a mutated run that ran nothing is never a verdict; a deletion whose block the head still holds is `block-present-at-head`; a test file the working tree lacks, such as one the head deleted whole, runs from a copy under the scratch directory with its imports resolved from its own path, and its vitest config comes from the base's `ledger.json`. The record is deterministic, and each listed block carries the classifier's `bodyHash` at the base and at the head, `null` on a side where it does not exist, which the CI gate of L45 reads. The tool does no git write, creates no worktree and installs nothing, so the verifier may run it. **The real run**, `--base 6b0395cd --head d6475fd0`: `inputValidation.ts` `.max(256)` → `.max(5)` against `should validate user names with spaces` is NO-GAIN, red at the base `should validate alphanumeric user names` and `should validate user names with spaces`; `subscriptionSchemas.ts` `.default(14)` → `.default(7)` against `accepts optional bundleId` is NO-GAIN, red at the base `defaults trialDays to 14`; `secureSchemas.ts` with `page`'s `.default(1)` removed, so `{}` is refused and the base form's `if (result.success)` skips, against `should apply default values` is GAIN, red only at the head. The head form of that block reddened, so the served head content went through the transform. Two runs of the same case file wrote records `cmp` reads identical, and each block's `bodyHash` equals the one `ledger.json` holds at that ref. Planted: `offset`'s `.max(5)`, which `should apply default values` does not exercise, is BROKEN, and a search the subject does not hold is UNDECIDED `search-absent`; the pair exits 1. **After the independent verifier's correction batch** the three verdicts are unchanged and two runs again write records `cmp` reads identical. The verifier's idle case, a rewrite of `mutationDiff.test.ts`, which `6b0395cd` lacks, is UNDECIDED `base-empty`. WU-2.8.2d's whole-file deletion of `trendAnalysisService.opportunities-report.test.ts`, `--base 2ee28005^1`, with WU-2.8.8's mutations (`region: options.region || "global"` at :471 rewritten to `"global"`, and `report.category = options.category` removed): `--head 2ee28005` gives LOST for `should respect region filter` and `should respect category filter`; `--head b750fe30` gives SAFE, red at the head `returns the requested region when given a region filter` and `returns the requested category when given a category filter` of `trendAnalysisService.init-trending.test.ts`. The verifier's WU-2.8.6b case, `storage-s3` `accepts files under 100MB` with files under 10MB refused, stays LOST; its base assertion was `if (!result.ok) expect(result.error).toBe("SERVICE_ERROR")` on a 5 MB upload. The verifier judges that mutation unrealistic, so the case shows the LOST path, not a proven loss: the realistic one, `MAX_FILE_SIZE` cut to 10 MB, is VACUOUS, because the block uploads 5 MB. **Checks.** `mutationDiff.test.ts` red first, the modules absent, then 69/69, and after the correction its 11 new or changed cases red first (11 failed, 67 passed), then 78/78; `hardProbe.test.ts` 91/91 with `sortedKeys` newly exported from `hard-probe-core.mjs`, its behaviour unchanged; `ledger.test.ts` and `testingMetrics.test.ts` 234/234; lint, knip (0 regressions), typecheck (the tests ratchet at 2006, its baseline), jscpd, the dependency ledger, the artifact-language gate, reach and `scopes` pass. Out of scope: node:test, a later unit (WU-2.3c), and any CI gate. **Review.** The independent verifier found one blocker: an idle base run (no scope file at the base, so no marker) read as GAIN. It also found three gaps: a scope read only at the head, a whole-file deletion refused, and a surviving block taken as a deletion. All four were fixed in one correction, and its re-check passed on each. The native review (reliability and resilience lenses) approved with two warnings. The first is taken: `showAt` read any `git show` failure as an absent file, which could drop a scope file and turn its red into GAIN; a file the commit holds that git cannot show now stops the run with exit 3, and the three A2 verdicts stay the same. The second is justified: each run writes its served log under its own `run-<n>` stem in a scratch directory made per invocation, so no stale entry can reach a later run. One suggestion is justified as the test seam's contract: the run timeout applies to the default runner only. Round 2 approved both commits with three suggestions. Two are taken, because each has a behavioural consequence: `presentAt` now reads the commit's tree listing through the fail-closed `git()` helper, so no git failure reads as an absent file; and a served file that never reached the plugin is `not-served`, with its own case, no longer folded into `not-applied`. The third, a repeated `it.each` label, is SB-342 in `docs/reports/review-suggestions-book.md`. The suite holds 79, and the three A2 verdicts stay the same. Round 3 approved the tool with three warnings and one suggestion. Each warning is justified because its path fails closed: an absent served log reads `not-served`, a timed-out run reads `timeout`, and a copied file with no alias reads `runner-failed`, each UNDECIDED and never GAIN or SAFE. The two untested paths are SB-343 and SB-344, and the suggestion is SB-345.

What the blocks read is a key or a type `tsc` refuses first (`Promise<ViralContentAnalysis>`, `Promise<ContentDiscoveryInsight>`, no `Record` or `unknown` in either, `turbo run typecheck` in the required `Lint and Format Check`), or the range of a literal: `sustainabilityIndex: 7.5` reddens only them, but that is an edit of an invented figure, and a test of it would pin what DEF-12 exists to remove, so none is written. `generateTrendReport` (11): region and category are LOST, below. Categorized content: COVERED by `trendRoutes.test.ts:306`; `trending: {}` (:482) makes the route's spread throw, a 500, and the 3 report tests of that suite go red. DEF-182, found by the independent verifier: a one-image body of 1470 characters with sentence breaks renders 2 slides, `planCarousel` attaches media to the first slide only (`contentHelpers.ts:159`), and `publishThread` refuses a slide with no media URL (`InstagramAdapter.ts:467`) before any API call. For WU-2.10, among tests the classifier calls BEHAVIOURAL (refinement 3): the `Story Publishing` and `Reel Publishing` blocks of `FacebookAdapter.stories-reels` build a `RenderedPost` and construct no adapter around it, the shape of the deleted :242; and `XAdapter.test.ts:173`, titled "exactly at 280 chars", runs 274, the fixture of the kept `XAdapter.content` :42. THREAD-BOUNDARY-UNDER-LIMIT, state uncertain: a body of 275 to 280 characters fits the declared 280 and `render` returns a thread of 2, measured at both ends, and no kept test pins whether that margin is deliberate.

The tests typecheck ratchet shrank one entry, `AbstractWebhookProcessor.test.ts` TS2345 2 → 1 (the `RelatedEntities` argument of the deleted :146's `process` call), and its `--write` lowered the baseline 2025 → 2024 errors, still 274 files with errors and 796 test files in the program. The ledger at `fe470b86` lists 59 blocks other than BEHAVIOURAL in 31 files there (54 EXISTENCE-ONLY, 3 EMPTY, 1 CONDITIONAL-ONLY, 1 TITLE-CONTRADICTION; twenty-nine rows REWRITE, basis `some-decorative`; two ADJUDICATE, `application/referralConversion` on `title-contradiction` and `infrastructure/HttpClientPort.contract` on `subject-not-imported`; no DELETE row). `probes.json` holds 78 probes on tests of these two directories, 2 of them on listed blocks: `application/referralConversion.test.ts` :259 RED and `infrastructure/HttpClientPort.contract.test.ts` :25 GREEN; a RED probe is a weak signal, since the probe makes the subject throw and a test that awaits it goes red whether or not it asserts behaviour. The subjects are use cases and adapters whose ports the tests mock, so each block was read against a regression of the use case or adapter itself (a dropped call or spread, a mapping that swaps or crosses fields, a rule that refuses what it accepted): would it turn the block red, given what the test builds? Findings for the Master Plan; the knip ratchet is clean, and the deletion takes no member's last reader (`rg` over `apps`, `packages` and `infra`): `PostRepository.bulkUpdateStatus` (`packages/core/domain/src/repositories/PostRepository.ts:124`, adapter `packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/post/PrismaPostRepository.ts:409`) has no production caller, read by tests alone, which the deletion did not change; and a residual of the gates: the plan-phase lines of `infrastructure/PrismaPostQueryRepository.test.ts` (P2-3) and `infrastructure/PrismaPostRepository.test.ts` (H12, with the Spanish word `FASE`) and the Spanish "y" of the `infrastructure/adapters/SlackNotifierAdapter.test.ts` header, corrected in this slice, stood at the base because fitness #8 does not match those spellings and fitness #53 counts no single word below its two-word threshold, and `application/customReportUseCases.test.ts` carries no `@layer` tag, which stays for WU-2.10 since adding a line moves its blocks. The native reviews of the deletion commit (reliability and readability) returned nine findings, each read against the tree: the `@layer infrastructure` of `integrationUseCases.test.ts` is the canon's value for every test file (CODING_STANDARDS §Mapping by context), whatever layer its subjects belong to; the inbox header names the file's four describes (`IngestSocialMessage`, `SendReply`, `MarkMessageRead`, `AssignMessage` at :96, :298, :557, :624), and the three use cases the old header listed are tested nowhere in it; the recurring header names what its seven tests assert, and no test of parent ownership exists in the file (the old claim was false at the base); the `CachePort-DI` header says the token is wired by the suite's own factory, which is the local-container fact the finding asks to make explicit, and the production registration is exercised by `apps/api/tests/cache.integration.test.ts` :104; the five HTTP verbs of `FetchHttpClient` are held by `implements HttpClientPort` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/FetchHttpClient.ts:25`) at compile time and exercised at runtime by `FetchHttpClient.test.ts`; the deleted `CachePort-DI` :52 checked `instanceof` against the adapter the suite's own factory registered; the Date mapping of `scheduledAt` and `publishedAt` is pinned by the kept tests of `PrismaPostQueryRepository.test.ts` that read the same `toReadModel` (the verifier confirmed :425 and :483 at the base); the deleted `bulkUpdateStatus([])` test asserted `ok` while the no-op its title claims was never asserted (`updateMany` still runs with an empty `in`), and the method has no production caller (the Master Plan row); and a resolved `{ ok: false }` delivery is never read by `TriggerIntegrationEventService.fire`, whose `Promise.allSettled` cannot reject on it, while the delivery that rejects is the kept EMPTY :479's fixture, which the catch handles. The readability review of the documents commit returned two findings on the one-line formats of the As-of and of this entry, which keep their documents' shape, as every earlier slice's entries do.

- 2026-10-29 — WU-2.5b d1, `PR deld1` (`workstream/tr-deletion-record`) — the ledger records what a demolition slice deleted, so a landed slice keeps #52 green: a slice of `slices.json` may add `deleted: { files, blocks }`, its drop as its pull request records it, frozen and never recomputed from rows, and `floors: { <metric>: { from, to } }`, descents of #37's four floors (floors without a deletion are refused). A path has left the tree when `git ls-files` no longer lists it, never merely because the ledger holds no row for it, and it must have the shape of a ledger row. A deleted slice's paths may have left the tree: a whole-file slice, one that records every file it lists deleted, refuses every path still tracked, a row or not, by name, and a slice that keeps some of its files must record as deleted exactly its paths that left the tree. Nothing proves such a path was ever a test (the ledger reads no history), so the deletion pull request's review reads the list, a residual of #52. `summary.deleted` is the sum of the recorded drops. The overlay holds only live rows: an `adjudications.json` entry for a path a slice records deleted is refused, saying to remove it. `LEDGER.md` gives a deleted slice its `## Slice` section with the recorded drop, the floors lowered, a table of the files it deleted and the rows it keeps; with no deleted slice, both outputs are byte-identical to the base's (the differential of `--check`, `--area` and the write mode). A slice with an unknown key now says `has unknown keys …` instead of the generic shape message. Block-level `deleted.blocks` for a slice that keeps its files is accepted and derived by nothing, a residual of #52. `ledger.test.ts` 184 → 209 cases, no new test file (M1 unchanged); the regenerated ledger moves one row, `ledger.test.ts` 77 → 94 blocks (15200 → 15217). ## How to extend

### Add a work unit

1. Add its row to [§Work units](#work-units) at ⬜, in its phase, with the same id the plan uses.
2. Add it to [§Plan (fixed)](TESTING_REFOUNDATION_PLAN.md#plan-fixed) ONLY as a re-plan — that is, together with a row in
   [§Decisions log](#decisions-log) naming the fork, the options, the chosen option and the date. The
   fixed plan is not edited to match what happened.
3. If the work unit adds or modifies a gate, its pull request also adds the [§Gates](#gates) row with
   the demonstrated red, and moves M7.

### Move a metric

1. Move it in the pull request that moved it — never in a separate tracker pull request; a
   [re-plan](#re-plan) is the one exception.
2. Update `Now`, fill `Moved by` with that pull request's alias, and refresh the `As of` line, which
   moves with the last pull request that moved a row. Every unit that moves a row, a metric or a gate
   adds one line at the top of [§Log](#log), newest first:
   `- YYYY-MM-DD — <unit, alias, branch> — #<PR> (<merge sha>) — <what moved>`.
3. If the value comes from a derivation, re-run `scripts/testing/metrics.mjs --m<N>` and use its output
   verbatim. If it comes from a run, name the run id in `Re-derive with`.
4. A new metric MUST name a command that produces it. A metric with an empty `Re-derive with` is
   rejected.

### Add a metric

1. Add the derivation to `scripts/testing/metrics.mjs` with its source class (`derived`, `network`,
   `pasted`, or `unavailable` with a reason) and extend
   `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/testingMetrics.test.ts`: the expected derived set is asserted there, so
   a derivation that silently degrades fails the test instead of going quiet.
2. Add the row here with its baseline measured at the commit that adds it.

### Declare a gap

1. Add the row to [§Declared gaps](#declared-gaps) with why not now and the owning work unit.
2. Do NOT represent it in the tree as a skipped test, a `todo`-marked test, or a test that cannot
   fail — the skip gates make that red, and that is the point.

### Re-plan

1. Write the row in [§Decisions log](#decisions-log) first: the fork, the options, the chosen option,
   who decided, the date.
2. Then edit [§Plan (fixed)](TESTING_REFOUNDATION_PLAN.md#plan-fixed) in the same pull request, so the two cannot drift.
